Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00367 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application is for a recently annexed property to change from County LI Light 
Industrial to LI Light Industrial. This zone change was submitted with an expedited preliminary 
plat application. As stated, the property was recently annexed by the Governing Body and this 
zone change is required to give the property a City zone district prior to any development. 
 
PlanCheyenne has designated these two parcels as Mixed-Use Residential, which is designed for 
residential uses as well as commercial and light trade mixed throughout. The proposed zone 
district of LI would be able to fit into the Mixed-Use Residential land use, as LI has some 
restrictions on it that HI Heavy Industrial does not. With the property currently being zone 
County LI, a change to LI would create continuity through the annexation. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC), staff recommends approval of the zone change, advancing 
it to the Governing Body. 
 
 
 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00367 
4 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria: 
 
PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
Mixed-Use Residential is the designation given to the subject area within ShapeCheyenne, which 
has the future land use map within PlanCheyenne. Mixed-Use Residential areas are intended for a 
variety of housing options, diversifying both density and type of housing units. As a secondary 
use, Mixed-Use Residential also should include a mix of retail, offices, and light trade that are 
related to the neighborhood. The LI Light Industrial zone district can work within the Mixed-Use 
Residential area. LI Light Industrial has more restrictions on the uses allowed compared to HI 
Heavy Industrial. The restrictions include not allowing some uses as well as certain uses requiring 
more conditional use permits. 
 
SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing 
zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance of 
that plan for these areas.  
 
The existing zoning is County LI Light Industrial. A designation of LI Light Industrial would 
maintain this consistency. As previously discussed, the LI Light Industrial zone district is able to 
fit within the context of the Mixed-Use Residential future land use designation and is therefore 
compatible with the comprehensive plan.  
 
ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according to 
the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property 
for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district;  
 
Since the property was recently annexed into the City, it must be given a City zone district before 
it can be developed. Currently, the property is zone County LI Light industrial. Under the 
proposed LI Light Industrial zone district, the property would be able to be developed according 
to the standards of that district as laid out by the Cheyenne UDC. 
 
STREETS & OPEN SPACE - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning;  
 
The property should be able to be developed according to the standards in Article 4 of the UDC. 
No new right-of-way is being proposed with this project. At the time of development, any 
accesses or internal streets would be subject to the requirements of Article 4 and reviewed with 
the site plan. Open Space will be further evaluated at the time of Site Plan Review, at which time 
any requirements or exceptions thereto will be determined. 
 
ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00367 
5 
 
The LI Light Industrial zone district is intended for controlled manufacturing or intensive 
employment that may have a secondary commercial use. This property would be able to meet 
that intent within the context of a Mixed-Use Residential land use in the area. The property can 
also meet the applicability by being located on a large piece of land that can accommodate the 
requirements for industrial uses.  The parcel should be able to achieve the design objectives for 
development within the LI Light Industrial zone. 
 
ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
Being located on a large parcel, this area should be able to meet the standards of the district 
when it is sold for development. As of the submittal of the application, there was no planned 
development. The annexation, platting, and zone change have all been happening to prepare the 
property for sale and development.

o 
Industrial development on this parcel may be problematic due to the existing truck

Dr./UPRR crossing to the north. We have documented instances of the crossing being 
blocked for more than 8 hours a day. 
-

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00421 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This application for a zone change from CB Community Business to MR-2 Medium Density 
Residential-Developing for property at the southeast corner of US 30 and Whitney Road. The 
application was submitted with a final plat application as well as a future land use map 
amendment request. A future land use map amendment was submitted due to the map stating 
this area should be developed as Community Business. According to W.S. § 15-1-601, zoning 
decisions should be made in accordance with the comprehensive plan. Amending the map would 
allow the map and zoning to be aligned. 
 
Should the future land use map amendment be approved, a change to MR-2 would be a 
consistent change in the area. With single family detached homes and twin homes immediately 
to the south and east, building more twin homes would be compatible with the surrounding area. 
If the twin homes are built, it is expected they will be built in conformance with the standards of 
the MR-2 district as well as the sub division standards of Article 4. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC), staff recommends approval of the zone change, advancing 
it to the Governing Body.   
 
 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00421 
4 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria: 
 
PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne recommends a Future Land Use Category of Community Business for the subject 
area. The Community Business land use designation intends for commercial land uses to be used 
and dissuades residential uses. Due to this, the applicant has also submitted a future land use 
amendment request that would amend the area to be Urban Residential. If granted, the Urban 
Residential future land use would be an ideal fit for the MR-2 Medium Density Residential-
Developing zone district.  
 
The Urban Residential district is designed for a variety of housing types and densities. The 
proposed twin homes would be a use that is described directly by ShapeCheyenne, the future 
land use portion of the Comprehensive Plan. According to W.S. § 15-1-601, zoning decisions 
must be made in accordance to the comprehensive plan. To change the zone district to MR-2, a 
change must also be made to the future land use map in PlanCheyenne. 
 
SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing 
zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance of 
that plan for these areas.  
 
The application is not consistent with the uses currently recommended in PlanCheyenne but 
should the future land use map be change, the application would be consistent. The area was 
originally laid out with commercial in mind but due to market forces in the area, commercial uses 
have not developed in the area as hoped by the owner. Converting the area to residential 
townhomes would be compatible with the surrounding area as are there twin homes and 
detached single family homes immediately to the south and east.  
 
ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according to 
the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property 
for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district;  
 
Under the existing zone district, residential uses are not allowed. For twin homes to be built on 
this property, the zone district must be changed to a zone that allows for residential uses. The 
proposed MR-2 zone district would allow for the twin homes to be built. It is expected the twin 
homes would be built according to all standards of the MR-2 zone district. 
 
STREETS & OPEN SPACE - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning;  
 
This Zone Change request has been submitted in conjunction with a replat. This replat, if 
approved, would allow for the area to be developed as residential within the standards of Article 
4. New rights-of-way are being proposed to serve the new twin homes and it is expected that 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00421 
5 
these rights-of-way would be constructed to meet current standards. Open Space is being 
evaluated with the Final Plat application that was also submitted. In short, this development 
would meet exceptions that are available within the UDC for requiring additional open space in 
the neighborhood due to sufficient open space already being provided. 
 
ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
 
The MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing zone district is intended for a variety housing 
densities and types. The proposed twin homes would be able to meet the Intent and Applicability 
of the MR-2 district. Design standards do not currently exist within the MR-2 district for single 
family residential units. 
 
ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
This zone change request is dependent on the recordation of the associated plat. The associated 
plat would replat the area out for ninety (90) residential lots. If this development falls through, 
the plat and zone change would allow the area to be purchased by another developer and make 
it ready to build housing units.

-

o

o

comments) that it is not interested in Lot 1, Block 1 without amenities being included. 
Please contact Parks & Rec to clear up. 
o 
Plat Note #2: Lot 1, Block 2, is to be donated to the Engineering Department for 
drainage purposes. If listed on the zone change map, please correct.

Section 1. 
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows:

That WJE, LLC, owner of the parcel of land being situated in a portion of the E½ 
of Section 25, T. 14N., R. 66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, has

Agricultural and Rural Residential – by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

Saddle Ridge, 8th Filing Annexation 
UDC-15-00422 
 
P a g e | 3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: 
 
This annexation application is for about sixty-five (65) acres of land north of and adjacent to E. 
Pershing Boulevard, west of Christensen Road. This application was submitted along with a zone 
change application from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential to MR-2 Medium Density 
Residential-Developing. Eventually, a final plat application will need to come before you before 
construction can begin. 
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this sort of development and annexing it into the City. This is 
evidenced by it being designated Urban Residential in the future land use plan as well as it being 
located within the Urban Service Boundary. The City has also been actively involved in the 
potential development of Christiansen Road underscoring the expected City growth into this area. 
PlanCheyenne is also supportive of its location near existing and proposed schools as well as the 
proposed Greenway extension through the project. 
 
As with any expansion at the edge of the City, this development will impact services within the 
City. This can be seen with the Police Department’s comments relating to additional officers 
needed to continue to provide the same level of service. Similar impacts are expected with fire 
protection, sanitation services, streets, and parks maintenance. In general, there are a couple 
options with City services. These options include maintaining the same levels of service by 
making new investments as the City expands or to not make new, additional investments and to 
reduce the levels of service across the City. With regard to making investments to maintain levels 
of service, the options are for the tax base as a whole to make those additional investments for 
the new development, for new development to contribute to the new investments, or some 
combination of the two.  
 
With this annexation and subsequent development, it is expected that the area will be developed 
according to standards of the Unified Development Code (UDC). It is also expected that all 
utilities will be stubbed to the edges of the project to allow future development on neighboring 
properties to occur. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute 15-1-402, staff 
recommends approval of this annexation with the following conditions: 
1) All technical revisions stated by staff in the official comments are addressed 
(City Surveyor & Development Department) 
2) E. Pershing Boulevard from the current City Limits to the farthest point of this 
annexation be included as part of this annexation. 
 
 
 

Saddle Ridge, 8th Filing Annexation 
UDC-15-00422 
 
P a g e | 4 
PlanCheyenne: 
 
This annexation and subsequent platting and zone change are consistent with PlanCheyenne. On 
the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject area has been designated as Urban 
Residential. The Urban Residential land use designation is for a broad variety of housing types 
and densities. It also calls for development within the Urban Service Boundary, in which this plat 
is located. Urban Residential also contemplates complimentary uses being integrated within the 
neighborhoods to serve the community. This 8th Filing is near both an existing elementary school 
and a proposed elementary school. It is also near parks and trails to serve the neighborhood. 
 
 
ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the 
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)): 
 
COMMON WELFARE – An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and in the city or town. 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the residents of 
the development once it is built. By being in City Limits, the residential will receive access to City 
Fire and Police services. The residents will also be hooked to City Water and Sanitary Sewer 
instead of having wells and septic systems. 
 
PART OF THE CITY – The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would be constituted a 
natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town. 
 
This subdivision, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the west makes the parcel a natural and geographic park of the 
City. The residents will work likely work in the Cheyenne area and will be a part of the social and 
economic fabric that is Cheyenne. Christiansen Road is planned to be a major north/south 
connection that would tie this area into the employment center known as Cheyenne Business 
Parkway, which is also in the City. 
 
LOGICAL EXPANSION – The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the 
annexing city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of 
the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be available to this location. As with any 
expansion at the edge of the City, it will typically require the expansion of City services. This is 
referenced by the Police Department’s comment. Similar impacts are expected for services such 
as fire protection, sanitation services, street maintenance, and park maintenance.   
 
CONTIGUOUS – The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or 
town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. 
 
The parcel proposed for annexation is about 31% contiguous, all along the western edge of the 
proposed product. This meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. 
 
UTILITIES – If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to service the annexed area pursuant to W.S. 
15-1-4102. 
 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the parcel. 

Saddle Ridge, 8th Filing Annexation 
UDC-15-00422 
 
P a g e | 5 
 
CERTIFIED MAILING – The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected 
public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date, and location of the public hearing required by 
W.S. 15-1-405(a). 
 
 
A certified mailing was made to the required agencies on July 10, 2015. 
 
 
 
ANNEXATION REPORT DATA: In compliance with W.S. § 15-1-402(c) the following information is 
provided: 
(i) 
A map is not required for this report based on the absence of exercising the authority 
granted under W.S. 15-3-202 (b) (ii). 
(ii) 
The total estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the landowners by the 
City related to the annexation is approximately $0.00.  All improvements are deferred until 
time of development.   
(iii) 
Services customarily available to City residents include water, sewer, sanitation, police and 
fire protection. Development will require extension of water and sewer services, which are 
available to the subject property. Water and sewer services are provided upon application 
and payment to the Board of Public Utilities, with sanitation services occurring concurrently. 
Provision of these services is initiated by the property owner.  
(iv) 
Fees for water, sewer, and sanitation services are a variable amount for commercial units, 
based upon volume.  Tap fees vary based on the size of the tap required. 
(v) 
The current property tax mill levy imposed is 66.00 and projected property tax mill levy 
imposed by the city is 71.00. 
(vi) 
The infrastructure described in (ii) above includes improvements required within the existing 
boundaries of the municipality to accommodate the proposed annexation.

ENTITLED: 
“AN ORDINANCE PURSUANT TO SECTION 2.2.1 ZONING MAP 
AMENDMENTS, SECTION 5.1.2 ZONING DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED, 
AND SECTION 5.1.3 OFFICIAL ZONING MAP, OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT CODE, CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM  COUNTY A-1 AGRICULTURAL AND RURAL RESIDENTIAL TO 
MR-2 
MEDIUM 
DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL-DEVELOPING 
FOR 
A 
PORTION OF THE E½ OF SECTION 25, T.14N., R.66W., 6TH P.M., 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO BE KNOWN AS SADDLE RIDGE, 8TH 
FILING (LOCATED NORTH OF AND ADJACENT TO E. PERSHING 
BOULEVARD, WEST OF CHRISTENSEN ROAD).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner of the property described herein, has applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on August 3, 2015, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that 
is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned 
application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion 
of the E½ of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as Saddle 
Ridge, 8th Filing, is hereby changed to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing (See attached 
Exhibit A). 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, 
sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00423 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application is from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential to MR-2 
Medium Density Residential-Developing. This application was submitted in conjunction with an 
annexation application due to the requirement of changing the zone to a City zone prior to 
development. A final plat application will be required to come before you prior to construction. 
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this zone district. With a land use designation of Urban Residential, 
MR-2 is a zone district that is compatible with the goals for this area. MR-2, as a zone district, is 
designed for a wide variety of housing types and densities. Urban Residential also intends for 
there to be complimentary uses integrated into neighborhoods. With this project, it is near an 
existing school and a proposed school to the west. It also has an extension of the Greenway 
through the project. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.1.2.d of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC), staff recommends approval of the zone change, advancing 
it to the Governing Body, with the following condition: 
1) Submit a map with the Development Department’s technical comments 
addressed. 
 
 
 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00423 
4 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria: 
 
PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne recommends a Future Land Use Category of Urban Residential, which encourages 
a wide variety of housing types with complementary uses interspersed throughout. The 
complementary uses include an existing elementary school to the east and a proposed school to 
the west as well as parks/trails through the neighborhood. Urban Residential should also be 
located within the Urban Service Boundary, which this parcel is in its entirety.  
 
SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing 
zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance of 
that plan for these areas.  
 
The application is consistent with the uses recommended in PlanCheyenne. The single family 
residential development is similar to the residential development immediately to the west and is 
compatible with the MR-2 zone district that is present. It is also compatible with the 
comprehensive plan, which calls for urban residential type development in much of the 
surrounding area. 
 
ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according to 
the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property 
for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district;  
 
With the parcel also being annexed, it currently has a County zone district. Per code, a property 
must have a City zone district before it can be developed within the City. Because of this, the 
current zone is not suitable for development within the City. The proposed zone district of MR-2 
Medium Density Residential-Developing is much more suitable to development, not only because 
it is a City zone but also because MR-2 is the residential zone that allows the types and densities 
of housing proposed. 
 
STREETS & OPEN SPACE - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning;  
 
The parcel for this zone change should be able to meet the standards found in Article 4. The 
transportation network meets the standards for lots and blocks or is able to meet exceptions 
described within the UDC. For this project, open space is being approached on a regional 
perspective as described within the preliminary plat for Saddle Ridge, 8th & 9th Filings. The 
specific standards from Article 4 are reviewed in depth during the platting process. 
 
ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00423 
5 
 
The MR-2 district is intended for a mix of density and dwelling types and is applicable where 
urban residential development patterns are desired. The application is able to meet the intent 
and applicability of the MR-2 district. For single family residential development, there are not any 
design standards regarding landscaping and building materials and form. 
 
ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
A final plat application will be following this zone change. If this development falls through, the 
parcel will be able to sold and developed as residential lots and should not be hindered from

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2015-08-04_TotalExtraction_74038696.pdf
ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, A PORTION OF LOT 6, ALL OF LOTS 7 AND 8, AND THE 
WEST 12 FEET OF LOT 9, BLOCK 2, PLUS THAT ADJACENT 8 FEET 
OF VACATED ALLEY, COULSON ADDITION, LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING (LOCATED NORTH OF AND ADJACENT TO RAWLINS 
STREET, WEST OF MCCANN AVENUE).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
That Leonard Golding, owner of the parcel of land being situated in a portion of 
Lot 6, all of Lots 7 and 8, and the west 12 feet of Lot 9, Block 2, plus that 
adjacent 8 feet of vacated alley, Coulson Addition, Laramie County, Wyoming, 
has petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and  
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffery B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
registered in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MR 
Medium Density Residential – by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

Coulson Addition Annexation 
UDC-15-00440 
 
P a g e | 3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: 
 
This annexation application was submitted for a parcel of about 20,000 SF of land on Rawlins 
Street, west of McCann Avenue. This application was submitted along with a zone change 
application so that the property can have a City zone district. The purpose of this annexation is 
the owner would like to connect to City sanitary sewer. The owner has already hooked to City 
water, which occurred in 1994. 
 
PlanCheyenne supports this annexation. This annexation helps to address a county pocket in 
Cheyenne. Further development beyond connecting to City sanitary sewer is not planned at this 
time. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming State 15-1-402, staff 
recommends approval of this annexation. 
 
 
 

Coulson Addition Annexation 
UDC-15-00440 
 
P a g e | 4 
PlanCheyenne: 
 
PlanCheyenne has designated this property as Urban Residential on the future land use map and 
is found within the Urban Service Boundary. Within the Urban Residential land use, a desired 
characteristic according to PlanCheyenne is to connect to City water and sanitary sewer. The 
purpose of this annexation is to connect to sanitary sewer. This annexation is consistent with the 
goals of PlanCheyenne. 
 
 
ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the 
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)): 
 
COMMON WELFARE – An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and in the city or town. 
 
Annexing this property will allow the owners to connect to City sanitary sewer instead of using a 
septic system. It will also allow them to have Cheyenne police and fire services available to their 
property in the event of an emergency. 
 
PART OF THE CITY – The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would be constituted a 
natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town. 
 
This property is already a part of the City naturally, geographically, economically, and socially. 
This area is already developed and would be consistent with the surrounding area. 
 
LOGICAL EXPANSION – The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the 
annexing city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of 
the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. 
 
 
With the property being part of a county pocket, it is logical that the property is annexed. 
 
CONTIGUOUS – The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or 
town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. 
 
This property has contiguity on both its northern and southern borders, for a total of about 53% 
contiguous. This contiguity fulfills the requirements by Wyoming statute. 
 
UTILITIES – If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to service the annexed area pursuant to W.S. 
15-1-4102. 
 
 
A utilities franchise is in place to service this property. 
 
CERTIFIED MAILING – The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected 
public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date, and location of the public hearing required by 
W.S. 15-1-405(a). 
 
 
A certified mailing was sent on July 10, 2015. 
 
 
 

Coulson Addition Annexation 
UDC-15-00440 
 
P a g e | 5 
ANNEXATION REPORT DATA: In compliance with W.S. § 15-1-402(c) the following information is 
provided: 
(i) 
A map is not required for this report based on the absence of exercising the authority 
granted under W.S. 15-3-202 (b) (ii). 
(ii) 
The total estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the landowners by the 
City related to the annexation is approximately $0.00.  All improvements are deferred until 
time of development.   
(iii) 
Services customarily available to City residents include water, sewer, sanitation, police and 
fire protection. Development will require extension of water and sewer services, which are 
available to the subject property. Water and sewer services are provided upon application 
and payment to the Board of Public Utilities, with sanitation services occurring concurrently. 
Provision of these services is initiated by the property owner.  
(iv) 
Fees for water, sewer, and sanitation services are a variable amount for commercial units, 
based upon volume.  Tap fees vary based on the size of the tap required. 
(v) 
The current property tax mill levy imposed is 66.00 and projected property tax mill levy 
imposed by the city is 71.00. 
(vi) 
The infrastructure described in (ii) above includes improvements required within the existing 
boundaries of the municipality to accommodate the proposed annexation.

ENTITLED: 
“AN ORDINANCE PURSUANT TO SECTION 2.2.1 ZONING MAP 
AMENDMENTS, SECTION 5.1.2 ZONING DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED, 
AND SECTION 5.1.3 OFFICIAL ZONING MAP, OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT CODE, CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM COUNTY MR MEDIUM DENSITY RESIDENTIAL TO MR-2 
MEDIUM DENSITY RESIDENTIAL-DEVELOPING FOR A PORTION OF 
LOT 6, ALL OF LOTS 7 AND 8, AND THE WEST 12 FEET OF LOT 9, 
BLOCK 2, PLUS THAT ADJACENT 8 FEET OF VACATED ALLEY, 
COULSON ADDITION, CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED NORTH OF 
AND ADJACENT TO RAWLINS STREET, WEST OF MCCANN 
AVENUE).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner of the property described herein, has applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on August 3, 2015, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that 
is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned 
application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion 
of Lot 6, all of Lots 7 and 8, and the west 12 feet of Lot 9, Block 2, plus that adjacent 8 feet of vacated 
alley, Coulson Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to MR-2 Medium Density 
Residential-Developing (See attached Exhibit A). 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, 
sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00441 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application is for a property located at 3714 Rawlins Street that is being 
annexed into the City. This zone change application will change the zone district from County MR 
Medium Density Residential to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing. The owner of the 
property is requesting annexation so that he can connect to City sanitary sewer. A zone district of 
MR-2 would be consistent with the other parcels in the area that are within City limits. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.1.2.d of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change, advancing it to 
the Governing Body. 
 
 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00441 
4 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria: 
 
 
PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne has designated this property as Urban Residential on the future land use map and 
is found within the Urban Service Boundary. Within the Urban Residential land use, a desired 
characteristic according to PlanCheyenne is to connect to City water and sanitary sewer. With the 
purpose of this annexation being that the property can be connected to City sanitary sewer, this 
application is in accordance with PlanCheyenne. 
 
SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing 
zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance of 
that plan for these areas.  
 
The application is consistent with the uses recommended in PlanCheyenne. The application to 
change to MR-2 is compatible to the surrounding area as it would be changing the zone match 
the properties within the area that are already within the City. 
 
ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according to 
the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property 
for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district;  
 
There are not any plans for redevelopment. The purpose of this zone change is that since 
property is also being annexed, it must be given a City zone district. The property, already being 
used as a residential property, is suitable for the MR-2 district should there be any redevelopment 
in the future. 
 
STREETS & OPEN SPACE - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning;  
 
As stated, there are no plans for development other than connecting to City sanitary sewer. 
Should the property redevelop in the in future, it should be able to be developed according to the 
standards found in Article 4. 
 
ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
 
Since the property is already being used as a residential property, it has the ability to meet the 
Intent and Applicability statements found in the MR-2 district. There are no design requirements 
for single family homes found in the MR-2 district. 
 

Zone Change Request/UDC-15-00441 
5 
ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
This zone change is being submitted due to an accompanying annexation application. Should any 
redevelopment happen in the future, that future development should be able to meet all 
applicable standards of the MR-2 district.

Section 1.  
That the City of Cheyenne finds as follows:
a.
That William P. Pendley, Elisabeth Y. Pendley, Luke T. Pendley, and William P.
Pendley, III, owners of a parcel of real property located at 4204 Charles Street,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, being the South ½ Tracts of 105 and 106, Sunnyside Addition,
Fourth Filing, and situated in a portion of Section 27, Township 14 North, Range 66
West of the 6 P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, have petitioned, which petition is
th
attached hereto as Exhibit A and fully incorporated herein by reference, the
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to de-annex the real property described
herein pursuant to provisions contained in W.S. §15-1-421; and
b.
That the parcel proposed for de-annexation is depicted and described on the
annexation map associated with Ordinance # 4038, which map is attached hereto as
Exhibit B and fully incorporated herein by reference; and 
c.
That notice of the petition for de-annexation of the above-described parcel was
published in the Wyoming Tribune-Eagle on June 13, 2015, as shown by the
Affidavit of Publication, attached hereto as Exhibit C and fully incorporated herein
by reference; and 
d.
That the Board of County Commissioners of the County of Laramie, Wyoming, has
considered the petition for de-annexation and has prepared a report on the impact of
de-annexation of the parcel, which is attached hereto as Exhibit D and fully
incorporated herein by reference, with said report concluding the impact to the
County if the property is de-annexed is negligible; and
e.
That the de-annexation of the above-described parcel will not cause land remaining
within the boundaries of the City of Cheyenne to no longer be contiguous with the
rest of the City and will not create any area which is entirely within the City of
Cheyenne, but which is not a part of the City of Cheyenne; and
 
f.
That the owners of the parcel sought to be de-annexed shall remain liable for any
assessments incurred or levied while their property was within the boundaries of the
City of Cheyenne and for any and all levies necessary to repay any indebtedness that
was outstanding at any time their property was within the boundaries of the City of
Cheyenne; and 
g.
That dedicated rights-of-way, easements, streets, and other property or improvements
dedicated to or owned by the City of Cheyenne, or which inure to the benefit of the
public, are retained by the City and the public pursuant to W.S. § 15-1-421(d)(iv);
and 
h.
That the parcel proposed for de-annexation is currently zoned MR-2 Medium Density
Residential by the City of Cheyenne.

2
Section 2.
That all of that certain parcel of land described in Exhibit B, attached hereto, be de-
annexed from the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall no longer
be a part thereof. 
Section 3.
That the said parcel so de-annexed shall be zoned in accordance with zoning
standards, rules and regulations of Laramie County, Wyoming. 
Section 4.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this de-annexation
ordinance and map (Exhibit B) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of
Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer’s
Office to remove said property from the official City Map.
Section 5.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval, publication and
the proper filing and recording of documents in the office of the Laramie County
Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds.

ORDINANCE NO. _________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 2.84.100, CITY OF CHEYENNE
HUMAN RIGHTS ADVISORY COUNCIL, OF CHAPTER 2.84,
MISCELLANEOUS BOARDS AND COMMISSIONS, OF TITLE 2,
ADMINISTRATION AND PERSONNEL, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.  That Section 2.84.100, City of Cheyenne Human Rights Advisory Council, of
Chapter 2.84, Miscellaneous Boards and Commissions, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel,
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created as follows:
Section 2.84.100 
City of Cheyenne Human Rights Advisory Council
A. Legislative Purpose: The Governing Body recognizes that all persons living, working, or
visiting the City of Cheyenne (“City”) should have equality of opportunity; that the City is
composed of and welcomes diverse individuals, groups and communities; that the City
acknowledges and values the contributions of all its citizens regardless of race, color, religion,
national origin, gender, age, sexual orientation, gender variance, marital status, military status, or
physical or mental disability; that the City acknowledges that every individual should have an equal
opportunity to participate fully in the social, economic, cultural and intellectual life of the City; that
all persons should be treated equally and with dignity and respect so that the City is recognized as
a fair and just place to live, work, and raise a family; that the City is committed to comply with
applicable federal and state laws that prohibit discrimination in employment, public services, and
public accommodations; and that the denial or deprivation of equal opportunity is detrimental to the
health, safety, and welfare of residents and visitors to the City of Cheyenne.  
B. The City of Cheyenne Human Rights Advisory Council (“Council”) is hereby established.
C. The Council shall consist of not more than seven (7) members appointed by the Mayor,
subject to the approval of the City Council, to serve without compensation, except that members of
the Council may be reimbursed for approved  expenses incurred in the performance of official
duties.
D.  Each member of the Council shall serve for a term of two (2) years, except that three 
(3) of the initial members appointed to the Council shall serve one (1) year terms.  Members of the
Council may be removed at any time by the affirmative vote of two-thirds (b) of the qualified
members of the Governing Body.  No person shall be member of the Council for more than six (6)
consecutive years. 
E.  The Council shall adopt bylaws and other rules and regulations as may be deemed
necessary for the conduct of its meetings and for the performance of its duties.  Bylaws, rules and

regulations adopted by the Council shall be filed for public record in the office of the City Clerk.
F.  The Council may recommend the expenditure of public funds appropriated to the Council
for official purposes subject to approval by the City’s Governing Body. 
G. The Council shall have the following duties:
1.
Monitor issues, events and circumstances that are detrimentally affecting equality of
opportunity in the City of Cheyenne and periodically report the same to the
Governing Body;
2.
Survey best practices developed at the local, state and federal levels to protect and
enhance equality of opportunity and periodically report the same to the Governing
Body; 
3.
Develop and recommend to the Governing Body educational outreach projects to
promote and enhance equality of opportunity, subject to budgetary, legal and other
restraints applicable to the expenditure of public funds. 
Section 2.  No funds in excess of Two Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($2,500.00) shall be
appropriated in any fiscal year for the expenses, duties, operations and projects of the Council,
unless otherwise approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the annual budget ordinance.    
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its passage and publication.
